# Deviation register

Significant deviations of the port from tg's core logic or mechanics (D81, D82, D86), one line each. Plain C# adaptations made by `docs/ai/translation-rules.md` and optimizations that keep the behaviour are not listed. The code of each deviation carries a `// DEVIATION(DVn): ...` comment. Items marked [C0] wait for the owner's confirmation at checkpoint C0 (D94).

| ID | What | Why | Decision |
|---|---|---|---|
| DV1 | A runtime error does not end only the failing proc (DM returns that proc's `.` to its caller and the caller carries on): it unwinds as a C# exception to the nearest isolation boundary (scheduler entry, `Waiting.Detach`, the `fire()` call in `ignite`, timer callbacks, signal handler calls in `_SendSignal`, callback `Invoke`, per-item `process()`/`Life()`, `Initialize` in `InitAtom`, `Destroy` in `qdel`, world phases), which logs it and continues. tg procs that rely on `.` after a runtime get an explicit try/catch. Consequences in the component system: `TransferComponents` stops at the first runtime in `TakeComponent` (the `CRASH` of an incompatible transfer, `_component.dm:462`), so the components after it stay on the old parent, where DM would go on with the next one; a runtime in a component's `Initialize`, or the `CRASH` of a parent deleted during `Initialize` (`_component.dm:58-59`), unwinds out of `AddComponent` to the caller's boundary, where DM would end only that proc and `_AddComponent` would go on with the component. | Per-proc catching would need a try/catch in every translated proc plus DM-try tracking across `await`; runtimes are bugs, and the boundaries reproduce DM where tg loops over many callees. | Wave-0 decision 3; D86; [C0]; translation-rules.md section 9 |
| DV2 | `QDELING(x)` and `QDELETED(x)` test `gc_destroyed != null` instead of its truthiness, so a datum qdel'd at world time 0 counts as deleted (in tg it reads as alive, since `gc_destroyed` becomes `world.time` = 0). | Map loading and most tests run at world time 0. | D86; translation-rules.md 10.5 |
| DV3 | Tick usage is virtual and always 0 (since DV33, SSair's work charges a virtual usage; everything else still 0): `TICK_USAGE`, `world.tick_usage` and `world.cpu` read 0, `TICK_CHECK` is false and `CHECK_TICK` never sleeps; `REALTIMEOFDAY` and `world.timeofday` derive from `world.time`. `world.realtime` and `world.timeofday` advance with `world.time` from a start value the host sets (`DmWorldState.RealtimeStart`, default 0; the server with `REAL_DATE` takes it from its wall clock at its start and carries it over each reboot, and its replay log records it, D332), not from the wall clock, and `time2text` formats in UTC, the world's local time (a time-of-day value below 864000 lies on the date of `world.realtime`), never in the host's time zone. `MC_TICK_CHECK` stays literal (it still trips on `state != SS_RUNNING`). The rust-g clock follows: `rustg_time_milliseconds` and the other rust-g timers read 0 (so subsystem init timings are 0), and rust-g timestamps (`rustg_log_write`, `rustg_unix_timestamp`, formatted timestamps) come from `world.realtime` in UTC. | The core has no wall clock; results must not depend on machine speed, the host's clock or its time zone. | D31; translation-rules.md 10.8, 11.3; the wave-1 values package (`DmTime.cs`, dm-conformance.md) |
| DV4 | `length()` and the byte-indexed text procs (`copytext`, `findtext`, ...) count UTF-16 code units, where BYOND counts UTF-8 bytes; the `_char` procs count Unicode scalar values as BYOND does. A byte limit such as `length(name) > MAX_NAME_LEN` admits about twice as many Cyrillic letters. | C# strings are UTF-16; tg uses the `_char` procs for player text; Cyrillic is the second language (D17). | [C0]; translation-rules.md 5.6 |
| DV5 | When a synchronous context (`SIGNAL_HANDLER`, `SHOULD_NOT_SLEEP`, any non-async proc) calls a proc that actually sleeps, the caller continues at once with a default value, the callee runs on detached and a runtime is reported; DM would block the caller (tg forbids this by lint, so it is a tg bug path). | A synchronous C# method cannot wait. | Wave-0 decision 2c; waiting-model.md |
| DV6 | `world.fps` and `world.tick_lag` are constants (20 fps, 0.5 ds); tg's `world.change_fps` and the configured FPS are not supported. | The core tick is aligned with tg's 20 fps and time is a tick count. | D31, D81; translation-rules.md 10.8 |
| DV7 | SSgarbage's queue and hard delete are not ported: a qdel'd datum is never hard-deleted (BYOND `del` would null every reference to it); it stays in memory while referenced and is freed by the C# GC; hard-delete detection and logging do not exist. `HardDelete` marks the datum deleted (`gc_destroyed = world.time`, as `Queue` does), so a stale reference reads `QDELETED` and a second `qdel` returns quietly, as `qdel(null)` would after BYOND's `del`. Of BYOND's `del`, `HardDelete` runs only a client's `Del` (BYOND's `del` of a client disconnects the player, inside qdel as in BYOND; M1). `HardDelete` keeps tg's statistics but not its overrun warnings (`garbage.dm:307-316`, `log_game` and `message_admins` past `hard_deletes_overrun_threshold`; with tick usage 0, DV3, they could never trip), and `Queue` and the `QDEL_HINT_IWILLGC` branch of `qdel` unregister the datum from the world registry (object-model.md section 7). Likewise a datum held only by a proc var of a sleeping proc and not read after the sleep may be collected before the proc returns, where BYOND keeps every proc var alive until then; tg's one pattern that relies on it, the Master's stack canary, is kept alive explicitly (`GC.KeepAlive` in `Master.Loop`; object-model.md section 8). | Garbage collector internals are not translated (BYOND refcounting has no C# counterpart). | translation-rules.md 13; object-model.md |
| DV8 | `/datum/component/proc/_JoinParent` inserts the component itself (`src`) where tg's `components_of_type.Insert(J, I)` (`datums/components/_component.dm:112`) inserts the type key `I`, when an exact-type component goes before a component of a subtype in the parent's per-type list. tg's line leaves a typepath in the parent's component list, which `GetComponent` and `GetComponents` then return in place of a component (a tg bug). | A list of components must hold components; the faithful line breaks every later lookup of that type on the parent. | Lead decision (translation rules v1.2, pilot finding); the code carries `// DEVIATION(DV8)`; ComponentLookupTests pin the fixed behaviour |
| DV9 | `/datum/controller/global_vars` does not check its `InitGlobal*` procs against its vars: tg's `New` copies the builtin vars of a fresh controller (`exclude_these.vars.Copy()`, `controllers/globals.dm:16-21`) and `Initialize` warns when `typesof(/datum/controller/global_vars/proc)` and `vars.len - gvars_datum_in_built_vars.len` differ, then logs the missing names (`:52-60`). `vars` is not translated (rules 10.7), so `gvars_datum_in_built_vars` and the count check are left out; `var/datum/controller/exclude_these = new` and `QDEL_IN(exclude_these, 0)` stay, so that world ids and timer ids match tg's. `typesof(/datum/controller/global_vars/proc)` lists only the procs the port has translated (W3.0, S3), so the initializers of globals that are not translated yet are skipped instead of raising "undefined proc". | VV bookkeeping that needs DM reflection; the check only warns. | Lead decision W3 G3; translation-rules.md 10.7, 13 |
| DV10 | The Failsafe is not ported: `/datum/controller/failsafe/New` only registers itself as `Failsafe` (tg's also clears `usr`, deletes an older Failsafe and starts its `Initialize` loop, which pokes the Master and recreates it when it hangs), and `processing_interval` is 0 instead of 20, so that the Master's `Loop` never finds it overdue and never replaces it (`controllers/master.dm:701-702`). A hung Master is not recovered. | The port's Master does not hang the way BYOND's can (runtimes unwind to boundaries, DV1; no tick overrun, DV3); MC recovery is not translated (rules 13). | Lead decision W3 G4; translation-rules.md 13; `controllers/failsafe.cs` |
| DV11 | `rand_seed(Seed)` does nothing: `/datum/controller/master/New` (`controllers/master.dm:96-98`) draws `random_seed = rand(1, 1e9)` and calls `rand_seed(random_seed)`; the port keeps the draw (it advances the world RNG as in tg) but cannot reseed. | The world RNG is seeded by the host and reseeded every tick (D31, translation-rules.md 4.13), so that every tick's draws depend only on the world seed and the tick. | Lead decision W3 G4; `Dm.rand_seed` (`src/Core/DmShim/Dm.Random.cs`) |
| DV12 | Emissive blockers are not created: in `/atom/movable/Initialize` (`game/atoms_movable.dm:170-203`) the branch that builds a `mutable_appearance/emissive_blocker` and puts it into `overlays` and `managed_overlays` is a deferred leaf (`Dm.Deferred`), and so are `update_emissive_block` and the other `blocks_emissive` paths; the `EMISSIVE_BLOCK_UNIQUE` branch (`new em_block`) stays `Dm.NotTranslated`. Movables therefore lack tg's emissive-blocker overlay. | Lighting planes are not ported (D24, translation-rules.md 13): the 3D client renders light itself, and `mutable_appearance` is not created in M0. | Lead decision D2 (wave 4); the code carries `// DEVIATION(DV12)` (package A) |
| DV13 | The busy indicator of a timed action (the cogbar every `do_after` of 1 second or more shows to others) is not drawn by the server: `/datum/cogbar/New` is a deferred leaf (`Dm.Deferred`), its `remove()` and `Destroy` are translated (with no cog, `remove()` deletes the cogbar, tg's own path). The progress bar its user sees is translated whole since the M2 integration (`/datum/progressbar`: its image goes to the user's `client.images` as in tg), but no client draws it: `client.images` is not replicated. | The 3D client draws the busy state from replicated simulation state (D86); the cogbar's overlays, images and animations are 2D presentation (`SSvis_overlays`, `image`, `animate`). | Lead decision D4 (wave 4); the code carries `// DEVIATION(DV13)` (package C) |
| DV14 | Every client gets tg's default movement keys: `client/update_special_keybinds` (`modules/client/client_procs.dm:1036`) reads the key bindings from the player's preferences, which are not translated (`client/New`'s preferences are a deferred leaf, M9), so `prefs` is null and tg would return with no `movement_keys` (no key would move the mob); the port uses tg's own defaults instead, the four movement keybindings of `datums/keybinding/movement.dm:5-31` (`W`/`North`, `S`/`South`, `A`/`West`, `D`/`East`) as `GLOB.default_hotkeys` holds them, and builds `movement_keys` from them by tg's code. | The M1 server moves players by tg's key path (`keyDown`, `keyLoop`, `client/Move`); the preferences and their keybinding datums come with the client preferences (M9), which remove this. | Lead decision (M1 integration; proposed by M1 P1); the code carries `// DEVIATION(DV14)` |
| DV15 | A capsule mob (a player's living mob in M2) moves continuously instead of by `client/Move`'s tile steps: the locomotion phase steps a kinematic capsule each tick at tg's speed, `0.5 / cached_multiplicative_slowdown` tiles per tick times `MOVE_SPEED_SCALE` (default 1, D106), the same in every direction (tg's diagonal step is about 6 % faster); `client/Move`'s gate runs as `capsule_move_gate` in tg's order without the `move_delay` bookkeeping (`modules/mob/mob_movement.dm:48-52`; the test at `:47` stays, as only tg's stops such as `Process_Grab` and a failed `resist_grab` push it for a capsule mob), and its tail (`COMSIG_MOB_CLIENT_MOVED`, the pull turn) after each crossing; `glide_size` and `move_delay` are not used for it; `dir` follows the view's yaw (4 directions with 5 degree hysteresis) where tg would let the mob turn (`face_atom`'s rule, or a move the gate allows) and the crossing `Move` passes `update_dir = FALSE`. M4c slice 6 (D146): a weightless capsule mob (the `ZeroG` driver) is moved by tg's whole `client/Move`, which the bridge calls as `keyLoop` does each tick the wish is held, and the capsule only follows `loc` with slides; that `Move` keeps the view's `dir` too (`client.dm_move_keeps_dir`), the client's `intended_direction` is the wish's BYOND direction, and `keyLoop` leaves capsule mobs to the bridge. Since the mob then keeps the view's dir, the jetpack's ion trail particle takes the step's direction (from the turf left to the turf reached) in `trail_follow/set_dir` while that Move runs, as tg's turned mob would give it (the space play test, 2026-10-15). A wounded leg's limp (`status_effects/wound_effects.dm`'s `check_step`, M6 slice 2) adds its pause to `move_delay` counted from now (`max(move_delay, world.time) + slowdown`), since a capsule step does not first set `move_delay` to `world.time` as `client/Move` does (`mob_movement.dm:107-112`). The gate's `COMSIG_MOB_CLIENT_PRE_MOVE` (2026-10-20, the confusion): its handlers may rewrite `client/Move`'s `direct` through `move_args` (tg's `args`, which its `. = ..()` then moves by), so the capsule's wish vector is turned to the direction they leave (`client.dm_capsule_direct`, `LocomotionSeam.TurnWish`); and since the signal is tg's hook of one `client/Move`, which runs once a move delay, while the mob has a handler for it the gate runs at that cadence and its answer and direction hold until the next (as DV69's weightless gate), so a confusion rolls its scramble once a tg step. The client predicts the straight wish and is corrected. The wind's resistance (`living.dm:1115`, `client.move_delay` ahead of now: the player is moving) counts a player walking the capsule (a held wish the gate lets move, `LocomotionSeam.Steers`) instead (2026-10-26). | The first-person 3D controls (D105); every tile hook still fires because each tile crossing is tg's real `Move`. | D105, D106; owner M2 P4 (`src/Core/Tg/Infrastructure/Locomotion/`, `src/Core/Motion/`); the code carries `// DEVIATION(DV15)` |
| DV16 | Standing players share 2 m tiles and do not swap or push: the crossing `Move` of a capsule mob carries `PASSMOB` for that call (tg's own mob-swap trick, `living.dm:232-245`), so `/turf/Enter` never bumps a mob; capsules collide with standing capsules instead (lying ones are left out, as tg's `TRAIT_UNDENSE`), and a contact runs `MobBump`'s signals only (`COMSIG_MOB_BUMP` and its kin, `living.dm:176-274`): tg's swap of non-combat mobs (`can_mobswap_with`) and the combat-mode walk-push (`PushAM` of a mob) do not happen between capsule mobs. | D51 (solid players, shove to pass) and D105 (Q1 answered "solid, signals only"); tg's one-mob-per-tile rule would make a 0.7 m body block a 2 m square. | D105; owner M2 P4; `// DEVIATION(DV16)` |
| DV17 | A shove (`/mob/living/proc/disarm`, `living_defense.dm:802`) also works when the shover and the target stand on one turf: `can_disarm`'s same-turf refusal (`:889-890`), and the same refusal in the species' `disarm` that a human's hand click goes through first (`_species.dm:992`), do not apply to two capsule mobs, the shove direction is the direction of the two capsule centres by the bridge's 8-sector rule (a cardinal unless the offset is more than 22.5 degrees off its axis: the rule of the wish direction and of the facing, `LocomotionSeam.SectorDir`; lead decision at the M2 integration) instead of `get_dir` of the two turfs, and the destination is the target's turf plus one step in that direction; the target still moves tg's full tile (2 m), slid over its glide time. | Under DV16 two players a metre apart often share a 2 m tile (D105, Q2). | D105; owner M2 P1 (`disarm`, `can_disarm`) with M2 P4's capsule centres; `// DEVIATION(DV17)` |
| DV18 | `/atom/IsObscured` (`_onclick/click.dm:192`) reads the atom's own turf (`get_turf(src)`) where tg reads `get_turf_pixel(src)`, the turf under the atom's drawn sprite (shifted by its pixel offsets and icon size): the `PREVENT_CLICK_UNDER_1` blockers of the turf the atom stands on decide whether a click on it is obscured. For a 32x32 atom with no pixel offset, every M2 mob, the two are the same turf. | In 3D a click is a camera ray that hits the atom itself, not a pixel of a 2D map drawn onto a neighbouring tile; `get_turf_pixel` also needs the `.dmi` metadata on the server, which it does not load (D104). | D105, D104; proposed by M2 P4, assigned at the M2 integration; `// DEVIATION(DV18)` in `_onclick/click.cs` |
| DV19 | GAGS icons are composed on the client: `SSgreyscale.GetColoredIconByType` (`controllers/subsystem/processing/greyscale.dm:85`) returns the text `gags:<config type path>\|<colours>` instead of `configurations[type].Generate(colors)`, and `SSgreyscale` stays an inert stub (no `Initialize`, no JSON configurations on the server). The client (M2 P7's `GagsComposer`) reads tg's configuration JSON and runs the same layer and blend steps; BYOND's exact rounding of partially transparent pixels is not reproduced (straight-alpha integer Porter-Duff, rounded to nearest). The airlock overlay cache (`get_airlock_overlay`, airlock.dm:25) and the damaged clothes cache (`/obj/item/clothing/update_overlays`, clothing.dm:524) key a GAGS icon by that name, since a text has no `REF` (M5 slice 6: the material airlock and its assembly; M6 slice 8: GAGS shoes torn by a bomb). | The server renders no icons (D104: the client composes tg's own icon files); the result differs from BYOND's only in the rounding of semi-transparent pixels. | D104; M2 P3 (DV-P3b) and M2 P7, merged at the M2 integration; `// DEVIATION(DV19)` in `greyscale.cs`, `airlock.cs` and `clothing.cs` |
| DV20 | **Retired (M6 slice 7b, D156): the leg's `handle_masking` is translated, its two masked images an `/icon` Blend recipe the client composes.** Was: a leg is drawn as one image: the leg's `handle_masking` (`carbon_update_icons.dm:518-546`) does not cut it into the two `leg_masks.dmi`-masked images on `BODYPARTS_LAYER` and `BODYPARTS_LOW_LAYER` (a deferred leaf), so a human seen from the side loses tg's near and far leg layering. | The cut is a server-side `icon()` and `Blend(ICON_MULTIPLY)`, and the server renders no icons (D104); in 3D the billboard is seen from any side. | D104; M2 P3 (DV-P3a), numbered at the M2 integration; `// DEVIATION(DV20)` in `carbon_update_icons.cs` |
| DV21 | **Retired (M7 lumcount, D170): the sleeping tick reads the turf's lumcount as tg does.** Was: sleeping never counted as sleeping in the dark: the sleeping effect's tick (`datums/status_effects/debuffs/debuffs.dm:271`) read `rest_turf.check_lumcount_above(LIGHTING_TILE_IS_DARK)`, and the core kept no lighting state, so the turf always counted as lit and a sleeper in a dark room did not get the darkness bonus to the sleep quality. | The server now keeps tg's corner lumcount (SSlighting, `modules/lighting`; the lighting objects stay out, DV85). | D24, D170; M2 P1, assigned at the M2 integration; retired in `debuffs.cs` |
| DV22 | Space is not fullbright: a `/turf/open/space` cell is drawn as a black starfield and lit by nothing, and the hull and whatever stands on space cells are lit only by tg's starlight sources (the space turfs next to non-space ones, `space.dm:104-120`, at `GLOB.starlight_range`, `starlight_power` and `starlight_color`) and by ordinary light sources; tg's `/area/space` base lighting (`base_lighting_alpha = 255`, `COLOR_STARLIGHT`, `areas/misc.dm:7-9`) is replicated as it is but not applied to space-turf cells by the client's light grid. | A fullbright void around a first-person station hides the hull's shape and the starfield; tg's 2D view needs it, a 3D view does not (owner question 9 of the M3 scout). | D116; docs/ai/specs/light-grid.md section 3 |
| DV23 | A dev command `access WHO all\|none\|ACCESS...` gives a mob a dev access list that tg's `GetAccess()` adds to (one engine field of `/mob`), standing in for the ID card in the ID slot that tg's human `GetAccess` reads: airlocks and windoors check tg's `req_access`/`req_one_access` against it with tg's own `allowed`/`check_access` (`door.dm:327`, `modules/jobs/access.dm`). Retired in M4a with `/obj/item/card/id` and its trims. **Retired (M4a P5, 2026-10-09):** the command, `dm_dev_access` and the human's `GetAccess` override are deleted; a joining assistant wears tg's ID card with the assistant trim and opens Meta's maintenance and not security (`AssistantIdTests`); `give WHO /obj/item/card/id/advanced/debug` gives all access. | ID cards, trims and the ID slot are M4a (about 1.9k lines); M3's doors must open for the right people now (D73's dev tools). | D117, D130; the `access` dev command (M3 P3) |
| DV24 | What a client sees is decided by one threshold up to `SEE_INVISIBLE_LIVING` (25) for everyone, and per viewer only above it: the shared body carries every movable up to 25 whoever views it; since protocol 22 (D151) the observer layer (26 to 60, a ghost's `INVISIBILITY_OBSERVER`) goes to each session by its mob's `see_invisible`, as BYOND does. tg decides every atom per viewer, so a viewer whose `see_invisible` is below 25 still gets the body, and things above 60 (hidden pipes, cables and disposals under tiles, `undertile`: 100; landmarks and mapping helpers, 101) are never sent, even to a viewer that would see them (mesons, admins). | Every living viewer's `see_invisible` is 25; the ghosts need 60 (D151); the rest of per-viewer visibility is M10's interest management (D23, D40). | D115, D151; `ReplicatedFields.MaxEntityInvisibility`, `MaxLayerInvisibility`, `Replicator.Layer.cs` |
| DV25 | Boot loops that create one object of every subtype skip the types no translator has touched yet: `Master/New`'s subsystem list, `init_blood_types` and `init_species_prototypes` create objects only for the classes with a hand-written part (`GlobalProcs.typesof_written`, `[DmGeneratedOnly]`), while tg creates one for every type. Lookups, type caches and `pick()` over the same lists keep every type, as tg does. SSmaterials' slot table leaves out the slot types no translator has touched (`generic.dm`'s weapon head and handle, the spear's and the mace's), and an atom that names one gets no effects from that slot: its material applies as on an atom without slots (the chaplain's whip null rod, `configure_material_slots`). | With every tg type generated (D111), a generated-only subtype would run its translated ancestors' code with its own data and reach untranslated leaves at boot; the world stays as M0-M2 built it until each site's owner translates or defers what its types reach and removes the wrap (`types-converter.md` 8). `SSatoms.setupGenetics` left it in gaps P3: every mutation type is translated; `init_language_holder_prototypes` in gaps E2c: the holders are data and all live, and a corpse spawner's skeleton (Lavaland) names itself from its species' holder at map load. | D111, D112; M3 P1; gaps P3 |
| DV26 | A player joining a map without late-join spawn points spawns on a job start landmark: when `SSjob.latejoin_trackers` is empty (Meta has no `/obj/effect/landmark/latejoin`: tg's late joiners sit in the arrivals shuttle's chairs, `arrivals.dm:31-58`), the host's spawn points (`MapSeam.LatejoinSpawns`) are the turfs of the `Assistant` job start landmarks (`/obj/effect/landmark/start`, `name == JOB_ASSISTANT`) in `GLOB.start_landmarks_list` order, tg's round-start spawns of the overflow job. tg's own fallbacks are `SSjob.get_last_resort_spawn_points` (`job.dm:767-783`): a chair or a free turf of `/area/shuttle/arrival`, then the CentCom error room. Retired when the arrivals shuttle loads (SSshuttle's templates) or the late-join flow is translated (M10). | D108 loads the station level only: neither the arrivals shuttle template nor the CentCom level exists, so tg's chain ends in the missing error room. | D108; M3 P2, accepted by the lead; `// DEVIATION(DV26)` in `Infrastructure/MapLoader/MapSeam.cs` (`LatejoinSpawns`) |
| DV27 | An item, structure or machine whose own tg code for an interaction proc is not translated yet is refused instead of running its parent's code: a click, pick-up, equip, drop, throw impact, item use or storage action that would reach a type's own tg override of `attack_hand`, `attack_self`, `melee_attack_chain`, `attackby`, `item_interaction`, `interact_with_atom`, `tool_act`, `pickup`, `equipped`, `dropped`, `throw_impact`, `hitby`, the alt and ctrl clicks or their `_secondary`s (`InertSeam.GuardedProcs`) shows tg's balloon alert "not ported yet" and does nothing, where tg runs the type's own code. Items whose types override none of them (paper, toys) pick up, drop, throw, store and equip by tg's base item code; clothing does since the M4a clothing step translated its own `equipped` and `dropped` (2026-10-14: every clothing type Meta maps or its closets hold). M4b slice 3 adds `fire_act`: fire reaching an atom whose type has its own untranslated `fire_act` (a match, a flare, a fuel tank, a paper bin, ...) does nothing to it, silently (no user to tell). M4c slice 3 adds `ui_action_click` (an item action button whose item's own button code is not translated, a water tank's mister or a jetpack until its slice) and the action datums themselves: a button whose `/datum/action` subtype is inert (its own `Trigger`, `do_effect` or `IsAvailable` not translated: organ, spell and antagonist actions) refuses its click with the same balloon. M4b's fire extinguisher adds `expose_reagents`, `extinguish` and `wash`: reagents (the extinguisher's water) reaching an atom whose type has its own untranslated `expose_reagents` (a fish, a hydroponics tray, a spider) leave it alone, and water reaching an object whose type has its own untranslated `extinguish` or `wash` (a lighter, a cigarette, a flare, a bonfire, an organ or bodypart lying on the floor) does not put it out or wash it, silently. M5 slice 1 (atom integrity) adds the damage procs: damage reaching an atom whose type has its own tg `take_damage`, `run_atom_armor`, `play_attack_sound`, `atom_break`, `atom_destruction`, `handle_deconstruct` or `atom_deconstruct` that is not translated, an undeclared override or a generated live-partial stub alike (a stub would let the rest of the chain run around the missing part: a locker destroyed without dumping what it holds), does nothing to it (`InertSeam.MissingDamageOverride`, at the top of `take_damage`, of `/obj/hitby`'s and `attacked_by`'s hits, which show the user the balloon); a melee hit on a type whose own `attacked_by` is not translated is refused the same way; a repair (`repair_damage`, `atom_fix`) and obj's `deconstruct` (`handle_deconstruct`, `atom_deconstruct`) likewise, and `modify_max_integrity` does not break such a type. M5 slice 6 (airlocks) translates `/obj/machinery/handle_deconstruct` and adds a machine's own `on_deconstruction` to the guarded procs; M5 slice 7 translates the machine frames (`spawn_frame`, D148), so a machine with a circuit is no longer refused for that alone. M5 slice 8 adds the crafting hooks: a crafted result whose type has its own `on_craft_completion` and a used part whose type has its own `used_in_craft` that is not translated (an undeclared override or a generated live-partial stub; a spear, a flamethrower, a pod door) are not called (`InertSeam.MissingCraftOverride`), silently: the craft goes on, its parts are deleted instead of being stored inside the result, and an inert result keeps its type's declared materials. M5x slice 1 adds explosions: a blast reaching an atom whose type has its own untranslated `ex_act` (an inert machine, a freezer, a mob other than a human: slice 3 translates the living mob's, the human's and the bodypart's) or `contents_explosion` leaves it, or its contents, as they are (`EX_ACT`, `__DEFINES/explosions.cs`), silently, and `/obj/ex_act` deals no blast damage to a type whose own damage procs are not translated (`MissingDamageOverride`). M5x slice 3 adds `boom`: a reagent dispenser whose own burst is not translated (the water cooler's tip-over) is not burst by the fuel tank's code when a blast, a fire or damage reaches it, silently. An inert instance of a subtype that reaches a translated class's own code needing what that class's `Initialize` made (D112: its init ran none of it) is refused the same way (`InertSeam.MissingInitialize`, found in the 2026-11-14 play test): an inert gun (the captain's antique laser, the cycler shotgun) does not fire or switch its mode and its examine names no pin, an inert reagent container (a soda can, a drinking glass, the CMO's hypospray) is not used in hand, and an inert computer (a laptop) that a blast or damage destroys is deleted without its own deconstruction. | 868 inert `/obj/item` types on Meta (and base types such as the PDA and the headset) are real instances of their generated classes; C#'s virtual dispatch would silently give them the nearest translated ancestor's behaviour, which tg never runs for them. | DV30 | A click on a carbon's picture selects the body zone under the crosshair as the clicker's `zone_selected` (through tg's zone selector, `set_selected_zone`, so the HUD doll shows it) before tg's `Click` runs; tg selects the zone only on the zone selector of the HUD. The client finds the zone from the body part images of the picture (tg's `get_limb_icon` states from tg's limb icon files, the hand images counting as their arms); a lying body's parts are found under its turned picture. Since 2026-11-24 (package T, slice T1) a mob drawn from body part images is hit by the crosshair's ray on its body alone, so a click, a throw or a hover never lands on its clothes, hair, backpack or held items (the ray goes on to what is behind) and a click on it always carries a zone; a mob with no body part images is hit on its whole picture. The hit body and its zones follow the body where it is drawn: turned when lying, enlarged by gigantism, raised or moved by furniture (a stool, an operating table, a bed's tile, a handrail). Since 2026-11-24 (slice T2) the zone at a pixel is the body part drawn on top there in the compositor's own draw order, so where the legs overlap seen from the side the near leg is found, not the far one. Since 2026-11-24 (slice T4a, protocol 100) looking also aims at tg's eyes, mouth and groin (`BODY_ZONE_PRECISE_EYES`, `_MOUTH`, `_GROIN`, which the server selects as any other zone, for a carbon target only): tg's own regions of the zone selector's `get_zone_at`, placed relative to the head and the chest as drawn in that frame (the head's top row `t` and middle column `c`: the eyes rows `t-4` to `t-2`, columns `c-1` to `c+1`, and `c-2`, `c+2` on row `t-3`; the mouth rows `t-6` and `t-5`, columns `c-1` to `c+1`; from the side the same rows, the three head pixels at the face's front edge; from behind no eyes and no mouth; the groin the chest's lowest four rows in every frame), in the untransformed icon square so a lying, enlarged or raised body carries them, and only where the head or the chest is drawn on top. On tg's human from the front they are exactly the selector's pixels. Hands and feet stay part of the arm and the leg. A limit noted at the designer's request (2026-11-24): because looking picks the zone, a gun fired from the adjacent tile with the crosshair on the mouth's pixels starts tg's 12 second `handle_suicide` (the gun at the head, then five times the damage) instead of firing at once; in tg the player chooses the mouth on the selector beforehand, so there it cannot happen by a slip of aim. From further away the shot is an ordinary one at the head. Since 2026-11-24 (slice T4b, protocol 100) the hover carries the zone looked at too, and the server selects it on a carbon before tg's hover (`set_selected_zone` with `should_log = FALSE`: a look is not logged as a manual zone choice), so tg's screentip names what a click there does (carbon_context's Headpat on the head, Hug on the chest, Pull tail on a tailed groin); tg changes `zone_selected` only on the selector. While any of tg's do-afters runs for the looker (`LAZYLEN(do_afters)`, tg's own test of a mob in a timed action) a hover's zone is ignored, so a surgery step or the gun at the mouth keeps the zone it started with (the designer, 2026-11-24); a click or a throw still selects its zone then, as before. The server keeps the last look it skipped and applies it, with tg's hover of that target again (fresh hints), in the first command phase after the do-afters are over, if the target still exists, is in view and is a carbon; a later hover, an unhover, a click or a throw replaces or clears it. In cursor mode (Tab) a hover carries no zone: the free pointer passing over a person leaves the zone chosen on tg's selector; a click there still carries its zone. Since 2026-11-26 (slice T5b) the player's own body is a target the same way while it is drawn for its own eye (looking down past about 52 degrees, slice T5a): hit on its body parts alone, the nearest thing under the crosshair winning, the part under it carried by the click, the throw and the hover in every mode (tg's own click on one's own sprite); otherwise the own mob is left out of the picks as before. | In first person the zone selector is out of the way of a fight, and aiming at a body part of the 3D picture is the 3D form of choosing one; tg's attacks still read `zone_selected`; a melee hit no longer rolls the zone (DV591). | The owner's play test (2026-10-09): aim at a body part and hit it |

The preferences are not translated (M9): `openToolTip` reads no HUD style, so a caller without a theme gets `"default"` (tg: the player's `ui_style` lowercased), and an item's tooltip reads the defaults of `enable_tooltips` (TRUE) and `tooltip_delay` (500 ms), where tg's null `prefs` would show none. | DV90 | A `/datum/weakref` also holds its datum (`dm_target`, not hashed; `weakrefs.cs`), so a datum that only weakrefs still reach resolves until it is deleted. Holding the datum makes `resolve()` depend only on `qdel`. | M7 lumcount (2026-10-21), a determinism fix; object-model.md section 7 |
| DV91 | The T-ray scanner and the scanner goggles' T-ray, pipe connection and thermal modes show their images steadily. | DV95 | An aimed shot hits a body where its 3D line crosses the body's drawn parts; a wall it dents is dented at the height its line meets the wall. For an aimed shot `can_hit_target`'s living branch is the crossing test (after tg's firer, faction and unhittable checks; a dead body is skipped unless clicked, as tg's): the picture stands at the mob's centre (its continuous position, plus its pixel offsets at the mob scale) facing back along the shot, in the frame the shooter sees (the client's billboard rules), and the crossed pixel is looked up in the carbon's body-part images (tg's `get_limb_icon` states, the hands as their arms, topmost first) through a committed table of the body-part icons' opaque pixels (`Generated/BodyMasks.bin`, D104 kept). A hit sets `def_zone` to the crossed part and `impact` skips tg's `ran_zone` distance roll; a miss marks the mob `impacted` (tg's never-double-hit list) and the shot flies past it to what is behind (a wall, another body). This replaces tg's direct-target hit, the prone auto-aim (`hit_prone_targets`), the lying skip, the fully-incapacitated skip and, of several bodies the line crosses on one turf, `select_target`'s random `pick` (the nearest along the shot is hit). A non-carbon mob (an animal, a bot) keeps tg's 2D rule; a body standing across two tiles is tested only from its own tile (risk R2). Since 2026-11-24 (package T, slice T2) each leg's two images get the masks tg cuts them with (`leg_masks.dmi`, its states now in the table, on their two layers) and the body is placed as the client draws it (its transform and `pixel_w`/`pixel_z`, past the icon square for a giant), so the server reads the part the client finds at every pixel. | DV96 | tg's spread is a 3D cone and tg's recoil turns the first-person view. The deviation tg's rolls give `aim_projectile` (the gun's spread, a burst's, the casing's variance) turns an aimed shot by its size about a direction the world RNG draws after tg's own rolls (`phi = rand(0, 359)`: yaw by `abs(d) cos phi`, pitch by `abs(d) sin phi`), so each gun's fan becomes a cone of the same half-angle; the draw changes the rolls after it against a 2D run. The projectile's own `spread` var (`fire`) stays a turn of the heading. tg's `recoil_camera` (mob_helpers.dm:212) is translated; its kick of the client's view reaches the client as a CameraShake of kind Recoil (protocol 34), which the first person turns into a rotation of the view instead of an eye shift: the push behind the facing pitches the view up, the push to its right yaws it, 3 degrees per tile of tg's offset, out in tg's time and back over tg's backtime; the kicked crosshair aims the next shot. Since 2026-11-21 (the owner: the anti-tank rifle snapped up in a frame, every other gun kicked like nothing) a gun's shot adds its own first-person kick to tg's recoil (`FeltRecoil`): its kind's by tg's type tree (an energy gun 0.4 degrees, an automatic 0.8, a pistol 1.2, a revolver 2, a shotgun or a rifle 3, any other 0.5) times the square root of its round's damage over the kind's usual round (0.6 to 1.6 times), 1.5 times with tg's WEAPON_MEDIUM or heavier fired with the other hand full; tg gives nearly every gun 0 (a ballistic gun's 0.1-tile `min_recoil`). The client plays a kick the shooter's way, whatever tg's times and easing (`GunKick`): one kick turns at most 15 degrees; half of it turns the aim itself (the view's own pitch and yaw: a burst climbs and the player pulls it down with the mouse), and that aim comes back by itself 0.15 s after the last kick, less what the mouse pulled against it; the other half punches the view on a critically damped spring that peaks after 36 ms for a light kick and 110 ms for a heavy one (10 degrees or more) and returns by itself. A heavy kick also shoves the eye back (up to 10 cm), widens the field of view (up to 4 degrees) and rolls the view; the held gun kicks as big as the view (up to three times), slower when heavy, rolled to the kick's side (`HeldPose.Kick`); the dev panel scales a gun's kick by its picture (`held_items.json`'s `recoil`). From 2026-11-11 to 2026-11-21 the kick went out in 0.05 s and back over tg's backtime, added up to 12 degrees in all. tg's dizziness chain (DV106) keeps tg's steps and easing. The camera shake switch (D70) does not turn the kick off. A homing shot (tg's `process_homing`) homes in 3D (the owner, 2026-11-20: the Abielle smart SMG sprayed everywhere): after each turn of its heading its line starts again at its point and height and its pitch turns toward the target's middle by tg's turn limit, and tg's homing offsets (`homing_inaccuracy_min` to `_max`, 4 to 10 pixels for the smart SMG) shrink to 0.3 of themselves, the body being the target, not the tile; the client draws a line that turns 30 degrees or less as the same flight, between the shot's own points of its last ticks (each new line starts at its newest place, ahead of the drawn shot). The smart casing's lock on a shot at a turf (`locate` of a mob in 2 tiles) skips the shooter, since a 3D shot at the floor or a wall is often that near them. The cone keeps only the server's share of tg's deviation (`spread_scale`, `ShotSpread`; the owner, 2026-11-20: 0.5 by default, 1 is tg's), since first person shows the angle as it is and a drawn body is narrower than tg's tile. The own crosshair is a shooter's while a gun is held (the owner, 2026-11-21; protocol 92, `GunSight`): four lines as far apart as the held gun's widest shot looks on the screen (`GunSight.ConeOf`: tg's largest spread roll, 0.5 x 1.4 x the gun's spread plus its `dual_wield_spread` for each other light gun held in combat mode, or 0.5 x its chambered casing's variance, times the spread share), opened by each own shot, and a hit marker when one of the own projectiles hits a living thing; tg has no crosshair. A homing shot hits the mob it homes on when its line crosses that mob's turf beside the drawn body (DV95's crossing miss), as tg's always does (the owner, 2026-11-21: half the smart SMG's bullets flew through the man). tg's lock reticle (`smartgun_target`) is shown on the mob the bullets home on for a shot at a turf too (the port had lost tg's line there), made inside it and in its `vis_contents` as a gun's firing effect, so the clients draw it on that mob, 0.4 m toward the viewer and turned to them; tg draws it on that mob's turf. | A fan in the map's plane makes sense seen from above; in first person spread scatters up and down as well (D177). tg's recoil spoils a follow-up shot by moving the view under a still mouse; turning the first-person view does the same to the crosshair. | D177, D179 (3 degrees a tile); `docs/ai/specs/weapons.md` 2.3; `ShotGeometry.Scatter`, `ShotGeometry.Home`, `ShotSpread`, `FeltRecoil`, `GunKick`, `mob_helpers.cs`, `Replicator.CameraShake.cs`, `CameraLayer.Recoil`; Core `M7x/AimTests`, `Client/Play/ShotAimTests` |

The client takes the exosuit for its occupant's body (the header's own entity is the mech, `LocomotionSeam.OwnBody`), predicts its capsule (the prediction starts over when the body changes, climbing in or out, so neither is a correction), and puts the first-person eye at the mech's head (1.8 m) with a heavier walk bob (`CameraLayer.InExosuit`); the mech is drawn at the tile scale, its pilot's 0.8. Phazon's phasing drain moves from `vehicle_move` to the crossing (`capsule_crossed`). The Ripley also takes its pressure's speed as a pilot climbs in (tg only after a `Move`), so its first crossing does not change the speed under a predicted step. The Marauder's zoom keeps its movement lock and sound but not tg's `view_size` change. | The owner (2026-11-07): exosuits move as smoothly and in every direction as people do, and the pilot sees from the mech's head with a heavier sway. | 2026-11-07 |

Every client draws the car and what rides it in the car's frame at the own prediction's time, moving evenly (`PoseSource`, `ReplicaWorld.TramDrawShift`); the car's static parts move in their chunk batch, whose culling box takes in the whole trip, and are planned anew where the trip ends. A client's aim says which render tick it drew the world at (protocol 88, `ShotAim.Seen`); the server turns it into how many ticks behind its world that was when the click is applied, at most 12 (0.6 s; `anomaly-rewind` in the click params, `ServerHost.AimParamOf`, `ClientSeam.ReadRewind`), and the shot keeps it (`obj_projectile.dm_rewind`). On every tick of its flight a rewound shot tests a capsule mob at the point it had that many ticks earlier, interpolated as the client interpolates (a jump of over 2.5 tiles is not): the crossing (DV95) uses that point and the flight under it (DV526), the mob is met only on the turf of that point (`scan_moved_turf` adds the mobs seen there, `can_hit_target` skips the mob elsewhere without marking it hit), and its body where it stands now does not stop the shot (`CanPassThrough`). Reach is tg's own test of a click, `IsReachableBy(user, held item's reach)` (`click.dm:171`): the next tile, a long-reach item's tiles (a spear: two), over a table, not past a directional window or round a wall corner; a ceiling cell's answer is the project's own (not tg's screentip) and goes bare out of the ceiling's range (`CeilingSystem.InReach`) for every living mob whatever it holds, the AI and a mob inside a mech or a locker included; the mobs below that keep tg's screentip are about tg's screentip for things and turfs. Every living mob that acts from where it stands (on a turf, or in a `loc` whose `AllowClick()` is true) is gated, whatever its state of the moment (cuffed, incapacitated, stunned, a cooldown, throw mode); the others keep tg's screentip, since tg handles their clicks by another rule: a mob that is not living (a ghost), the AI, a cyborg with no module out (`interaction_range`), the alien hunter while `leap_on_click` is on, and a mob inside a thing that does not allow the click (a mech, a locker). A held keybinding whose key is no longer down (the window lost the focus, a panel took the key) is released by the client. On a real server the prompt shows a thing's name only from the server's answer to a hover of the current look at it (an earlier look's answer is dropped); known limit: an answer still in flight from an earlier look at the same thing counts when it arrives during the current one (the message carries nothing to tell them apart without a wire change). | DV693 | The belt hotbar (ui-overhaul.md section 10, package 3; not in tg): a human has ten cells, 1 to 7 the worn belt's contents (a belt-slot item with a storage; beyond seven no cell), 8 and 9 the left and right pocket, 10 the ID slot, on the keys 1 to 9 and 0. Which belt item is in which cell is the port's state on the mob (`BeltHotbar`, `dm_hotbar`): a new belt's contents take cells 1 to 7 in tg's storage order, an item keeps its cell while in the belt or, taken from the bar, in a hand, an item put in another way takes the lowest free cell, and one that leaves both (or an out item put elsewhere) frees it; a slot's own item is its cell's (it wins over an item out from that slot). A press (protocol 100's HotbarPress) does one of five rules: an out item goes back to its cell; an empty active hand takes the cell's item; an item out from another cell goes back there and the cell's item comes to the hand (nothing if it cannot go back); a held item goes into an empty cell; anything else is refused and the client told. Every move is tg's own: the belt's `atom_storage.attempt_insert` with smart_equip_targeted's checks and "can't fit" message, the stored item's `on_found` and `attack_hand`, the slot's `equip_to_slot_if_possible`, and `putItemFromInventoryInHandIfPossible` for a worn pocket or ID item (tg's drag of it onto a hand; `attack_hand` would open a worn wallet); dead or incapacitated (tg's `if(incapacitated) return`), or while an inventory move (DV696) is moving one of its items, nothing moves. tg's action buttons' number keys (the port's own, 2026-10-29) move to Shift and the number. The bar (slice 3B) is ten cells of 48 between the hand boxes in three groups (belt, pockets, ID), each with its bound key's cap and the item's picture as the server sends it, the picture faint while the item is out in a hand and the rim red for a third of a second after a refused press; with no belt the seven belt cells are half-seen empty cells. The hand boxes and the cells are drawn with tg's own slot pictures of the player's HUD style, scaled to the box (ui-overhaul.md section 16): a hand tg's `hand_l` or `hand_r` with tg's active mark (no border of the port's own), a cuffed or missing hand's red border over tg's mark; a pocket's and the ID slot's own state as the server sends it; the belt cells, which tg has no slot for, the style's blank `template_small`. tg's pocket and ID slot entries are drawn as cells 8 to 10 and keep tg's clicks and drags; a click on a belt cell under Tab is its press. An inventory move into the worn belt (DV696) names a bar cell (1 to 7) as its wish, not a place in tg's contents: once the item is in the belt (by tg's insert, or already there) it takes that cell: an empty cell simply; a cell holding another belt item swaps with the moved item's own cell (an item out from its cell in a hand still has it); an item new to the bar takes the cell and the item there the lowest free one (or, with none, goes off the bar, where the Tab inventory's belt panel shows it); a wish for its own cell changes nothing; a cell whose item is out in a hand, or is being moved by a press or another move, is not taken: the moved item keeps the cell it got as with no wish (`BeltHotbar.PlaceInCell`). A press marks out the item it took at that item's cell after its waits, not at the cell it read before them. tg's belt, back and suit-storage slots are drawn and clicked only under Tab, with the worn slots of tg's toggled inventory, in the Tab inventory's character panel (DV694; before ui-overhaul.md slice 4A in a row over the bar's right end). a worn storage not in a pocket, whose click opens it, and also one whose click does not open it: boots with a shoe pocket, which tg's own drag takes off untied or knotted, where the click would tie them), by tg's drag of it onto that hand (`COMSIG_MOUSEDROP_ONTO`, drag_drop.dm:31, answered by `drag_pickup` or the storage's `on_mousedrop_onto`); a click that does something else instead (a toggle) is done as in tg, and the move is refused; the item then goes on from that hand by the rule for a held item, and stays in the hand if the target refuses it; a **held** item goes on as tg's click with it puts it (a storage cell's `attempt_insert`, a slot's `mob_can_equip` with tg's equip delay and "You are unable to equip that!" then `equip_to_slot`, a hand's `putItemFromInventoryInHandIfPossible`, the drop key's `dropItemToGround`), **from either hand** where tg's clicks use the active hand's item; a **stored** item is reached (`on_found`, as tg's opening of the storage runs it), picked as tg's `attempt_pickup` picks (the auto-pickup flag, `MOBILITY_PICKUP`, the heavy-gravity wait) and taken out by **its storage's own** `remove_single` (a trash bag's search, an evidence bag's reset, `attempt_remove`'s `dropped`, animation and removal signals); into the **active** hand that is tg's own click (ClickOn's `UnarmedAttack`, the item's `attack_hand`, `attempt_pickup`; the move is done if the item ends in one of the sender's hands), into the **other** hand attempt_pickup's steps with that hand (`remove_single`, the pickup animation, a throw ended, `pickup`, `put_in_hand`). Every target of a stored item is asked before anything moves, as tg asks it for its slot highlight (`mob_can_equip` without its warnings and delay, then "You are unable to equip that!"; `can_insert` with its balloon; a free hand); the item stays in its storage through tg's equip delay and is taken out only after it, so it is inside the mob in no place only between its storage's own removal and its target, within one tick. **What still differs from tg:** (1) a stored item goes **straight on to a storage, a slot or the floor with no free hand** (tg carries it in a hand between two clicks): `pickup()` and the hand's `equipped()` are skipped, and the storage's `dropped()` runs a second time where tg's hand would let go (`attempt_insert`'s `transferItemToLoc`, or tg's drop for the floor, which gives the drop sound, the pixel offset, `do_drop_animation` and `COMSIG_MOB_DROPPING_ITEM`); (2) **between the hands** no free hand is needed either; (3) a target that no longer takes an item already taken out (it changed during its storage's own removal: a trash bag's search) sends it **back where it was**, into its storage by tg's insert, last in it (and its `dropped()` runs once more); if that refuses too (a NODROP item), tg's `put_in_hands` (a free hand, else the feet); (4) every path asks the target again after each of its waits (the take-off click's heavy-gravity wait, tg's equip delay, the storage's own removal): still the sender's carried storage, an empty slot, a hand it has and that is empty; after tg's equip delay nothing is put on if the item moved, the slot filled or the body stopped acting (tg's `equip_to_slot` would put it on from wherever it went); (5) one move of an item at a time, a hotbar press included: an item a move or a press is moving cannot be moved by another meanwhile; (6) a **reorder within one storage** (a wished cell, tg's `mousedrop_receive` with its quirk that an item moved onto a later one lands after it; nothing for tg's numbered displays) applies tg's `can_perform_action` and the stored item's click checks (above), but not its `active_storage == src` check; (7) an abstract or hand item (`ABSTRACT`, `HAND_ITEM`: the slapper) goes nowhere but to the floor (tg's drop, which deletes a `DROPDEL` item); an item whose tg `pickup`, `equipped` or `dropped` is not ported is refused before it moves (DV27); a runtime error inside a move is reported as the world reports runtimes and counts as a refusal (an item it left taken out goes to `put_in_hands`; if that throws too, it is reported and the item is put on the mob's turf by a plain `forceMove`, reported as a runtime); (8) a stored item going to the other hand, a storage, a slot or the floor never runs the item's own `attack_hand` or `COMSIG_ATOM_ATTACK_HAND` (a conscious facehugger's leap, facehugger.dm:67); (9) the take-off click and the active hand's pick-up call `UnarmedAttack` as ClickOn would, skipping ClickOn's `throw_mode` (tg would throw), `next_click`, `check_click_intercept`, `TRAIT_NO_TRANSFORM`, `COMSIG_MOB_CLICKON` and the `CLICK_CD_HANDCUFFED` a cuffed click sets; the drag of a worn item sends `COMSIG_MOUSEDROP_ONTO` alone and adds the slot click's checks (the cooldown `next_move`) that tg's `MouseDrop` lacks; (10) a drop works from either hand, where tg's drop key drops the active hand's item. A move is refused (the client told) when tg refuses it, when the body is dead, or when the body cannot act as tg's own click for that step requires: a click on a worn slot, a storage cell or a hand checks tg's click cooldown `next_move`, `incapacitated` (ignoring stasis on a slot) and a mech (screen_objects.dm:203-209, :297-302, :657-663), not blocked hands; a click on a stored item (its take-out, whatever the target) checks `incapacitated` ignoring restraints and stasis, the cooldown, blocked hands and a location that allows the click (click.dm:115, :120, :126 with other_mobs.dm:17-18, :161: a mech or a closet does not); a worn item's take-off is the slot's click and then tg's own `UnarmedAttack` or drag, which refuse blocked hands by themselves; a held item's drop to the floor checks nothing of its own, as tg's drop key (keybinding/mob.dm:90-102), and `dropItemToGround` refuses TRAIT_NODROP; buckled is no bar. A request naming what is not the sender's (another's item or bag, a floor item, a mob, a turf, a hand it lacks) does nothing and is not reported. A belt item moved into a hand keeps its hotbar cell, out, as the cell's key would have left it. **The Tab notice** (protocol 100's InventoryOpen) opens the carried storages as tg's `open_storage` and `show_contents` do, at most once a second, without tg's screen and without taking `active_storage`: for each listed storage on its own tg's checks (`can_perform_action` with `ALLOW_RESTING` and `FORBID_TELEKINESIS_REACH`, `MOBILITY_STORAGE`, locked, with "closed!" for the back storage, a client, `display_contents`) and tg's `on_found` pass over its contents (an armed mousetrap snaps on the searching hand and that storage's opening ends there; its stun then stops the others' checks); a storage that cannot be opened is skipped and the others still are; the back storage, opened, then plays the animation and the rustle or open sound that those near hear, the others no sound; each failed `can_perform_action` says tg's own line, once per storage. A quickdraw storage does not draw, and another container the user has open is neither closed nor replaced, also when a find ends the search (tg would close it). **Examine** (slice 4B-2): a left Click with Shift alone naming, as a screen target, an item the sender's listed storages show now (directly in one, among the first 64, neither locked nor hidden: `CarriedStorages.ShowsItem`) runs tg's Click with it, so `ShiftClickOn` examines it as a Shift+click on tg's storage screen would, though that item is no screen entry here; any other click on a stored item, and one on anyone else's, is refused as before (`ServerHost.TryStoredExamine`). | First person has no storage screen to click while looking: the designer's Tab inventory shows every storage at once and lets an item be dragged between any of them (ui-overhaul.md section 12, "Slice 4B: settled design") | ui-overhaul.md section 12; `CarriedStorages.cs`, `BeltHotbar.cs`, `Replicator.Storages.cs`, `ServerHost.Network.cs`; Core `UiOverhaul/CarriedStoragesTests`, Server `Integration/StoragesGameplayTests`, `Integration/StoragesHostileTests`, `Integration/StorageExamineGameplayTests`, `Protocol/ProtocolV100StoragesTests`, `Client/Play/StoragesClientTests` |
